# ARCH001 — Local Development Architecture Audit

## 1. Current environment inventory

- Active workspace: `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-site`
- Existing repositories:
  - `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-site`
    - Remote: `git@github.com:BobSanDiego/teachers-net-site.git`
  - `/home/bobreap/projects/lessonbank-2.1`
    - Remote: `git@github.com:BobSanDiego/lessonbank-2.1.git`
  - `/home/bobreap/projects/birdmart-site`
    - Remote: `https://github.com/BobSanDiego/birdmart-project.git`
- Existing DDEV projects:
  - `teachers-net` — running
  - `birdmart` — stopped
- Docker/DDEV router: running
- Existing Teachers.Net DDEV URL: `https://teachers-net.ddev.site`

## 2. Existing `teachers-net-site` role

This is an active engineering repository and Job Center workspace containing:

- A full WordPress core installation under `wordpress/`
- Existing custom plugins:
  - `profilaxes`
  - `tnet-jobs`
  - `lessonbank-workbench`
- Default theme: `twentytwentyfive`
- Existing uploads directory
- DDEV configuration
- Job Center, Core Terms, Membership Taxonomy, and Community 3.0 documentation
- Multiple active project cursors and engineering handoffs

It is not a clean production clone. Its Git working tree currently contains substantial uncommitted Job Center and design work.

## 3. Risks identified

- Importing production into the existing project could overwrite or contaminate active Job Center work.
- The current DDEV project name and hostname are already in use.
- The existing repository contains custom engineering plugins that must not be replaced by an unreviewed production copy.
- Production database content may contain credentials, personal data, scheduled jobs, absolute URLs, and environment-specific configuration.
- Uploads and generated files could be large and should not automatically enter Git.
- Lesson Bank has its own repository boundary and should not be merged into a production clone.
- A production clone must not share the current `teachers-net` DDEV project or database.

## 4. Recommended architecture

Use a separate filesystem directory and separate DDEV project:

```text
/home/bobreap/projects/
├── teachers-net-site/       # Existing engineering / Job Center workspace
└── teachers-net-live/       # Proposed local production clone
```

This provides the clearest isolation for:

- Filesystem contents
- DDEV project identity
- Database
- Local hostname
- Git history
- Production synchronization
- Backup and rollback

## 5. Recommended directory layout

```text
/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/
├── .ddev/
├── wordpress/
│   ├── wp-admin/
│   ├── wp-includes/
│   ├── wp-content/
│   │   ├── themes/
│   │   ├── plugins/
│   │   ├── mu-plugins/
│   │   └── uploads/
│   └── wp-config.php
├── backups/                 # local-only database/file backups
└── README.md
```

The production clone should not be created inside `teachers-net-site`.

## 6. DDEV recommendation

Recommended DDEV project name:

```text
teachers-net-live
```

Recommended local URL:

```text
https://teachers-net-live.ddev.site
```

Recommended configuration should be based on the existing WordPress/DDEV setup but independently initialized:

- Type: WordPress
- Docroot: `wordpress`
- Database: separate MariaDB database/container namespace
- No shared `name: teachers-net`
- No reuse of the existing project’s database
- No production hostname or additional FQDN
- Keep uploads outside Git and configure them as an upload directory if needed

No DDEV configuration was changed during this audit.

## 7. Git recommendation

Keep the production clone separate from the existing engineering repository.

Recommended policy:

- Do not initialize Git until the clone architecture is approved.
- Do not place the clone under the `teachers-net-site` repository.
- Do not commit:
  - Production database dumps
  - `wp-content/uploads`
  - Caches
  - Logs
  - Temporary files
  - Environment secrets
  - Generated artifacts
- Version-control only reviewed code and configuration where appropriate:
  - Custom theme
  - Custom plugins
  - MU plugins
  - DDEV configuration
  - Sanitized deployment configuration
- Prefer separate repositories or separately managed code sources for the production clone and engineering code.
- Treat the production clone as a synchronized runtime copy, not as the source repository for Job Center development.

## 8. Content handling

| Content | Recommended handling |
|---|---|
| WordPress database | Import into the separate DDEV database; sanitize URLs, credentials, scheduled tasks, and private data first |
| WordPress core | Use a known compatible WordPress version; do not blindly replace the engineering workspace |
| Custom themes | Transfer to the clone; keep source-controlled separately |
| Custom plugins | Transfer selectively after inventory and compatibility review |
| MU plugins | Transfer and review because they load automatically |
| Uploads | Optional initially; keep outside Git; synchronize selectively |
| Caches | Exclude and regenerate locally |
| Logs | Exclude |
| Generated files | Exclude unless explicitly required |
| Secrets/configuration | Recreate locally; never copy production secrets unchanged |

## 9. Recommended deployment workflow

1. Maintain the production clone as a separate, isolated local runtime.
2. Pull production content only through an approved, read-only or export-based process.
3. Import a sanitized database into the clone’s separate DDEV database.
4. Synchronize reviewed themes, plugins, and MU plugins independently.
5. Keep uploads and generated runtime files outside Git.
6. Develop and test changes in the appropriate engineering repository.
7. Deploy reviewed code through an explicit release process.
8. Never deploy by copying the entire local production clone back to production.
9. Use database migrations or explicit database export/import procedures for schema and content changes.
10. Maintain backups and a rollback point before every production deployment.

## 10. Phased migration plan

### Phase 1 — Approval and isolation

- Approve the separate `teachers-net-live` architecture.
- Confirm the production access method.
- Confirm the intended WordPress and PHP compatibility targets.
- No changes to the existing workspace.

Reversible: entirely documentation-only.

### Phase 2 — Create the empty local project

- Create `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/`.
- Initialize an independent DDEV WordPress project named `teachers-net-live`.
- Confirm the new URL and separate database.

Reversible: remove the new project only after confirming it contains no required data.

### Phase 3 — Prepare transfer manifest

- Inventory production database, themes, plugins, MU plugins, and uploads.
- Record source paths, checksums, versions, and exclusions.
- Confirm backup and rollback locations.

Reversible: manifest-only.

### Phase 4 — Transfer and sanitize

- Export the production database.
- Transfer code and optional uploads using `rsync`.
- Sanitize the database and local configuration.
- Import into the separate DDEV database.

Reversible: restore the pre-import database and remove transferred content from the new clone.

### Phase 5 — Local verification

- Verify WordPress loads locally.
- Verify plugins and themes.
- Verify database tables and URLs.
- Verify no production hostname, credentials, mail delivery, cron jobs, or external integrations remain active.

Reversible: restore the clone from its pre-verification snapshot.

### Phase 6 — Ongoing synchronization

- Use scheduled or manually approved `rsync` pulls from production.
- Keep production-to-local synchronization separate from code deployment.
- Record each synchronization in a manifest or handoff note.

## Explicit transfer recommendation

When authorized in a later ticket, prefer `rsync` over FTP. The exact commands should be finalized only after the production SSH host, account, source paths, key path, and exclusion policy are known.

The eventual shape should be similar to:

```bash
rsync -avz --dry-run \
  --exclude='cache/' \
  --exclude='logs/' \
  --exclude='*.log' \
  PROD_USER@PROD_HOST:/path/to/wordpress/wp-content/themes/ \
  /home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/themes/
```

Use `--dry-run` first, then perform the actual transfer only under an explicitly authorized transfer ticket.

## Verification

- No files changed.
- No directories created, moved, renamed, or deleted.
- No DDEV configuration or state changed.
- No Git state changed.
- No production access occurred.
- No production files or databases were copied.
- Existing Job Center workspace remains in place and was not modified.

# SEC002 — Visible Mailform Trigger and Caller Trace Audit

## 1. Confirmed trigger inventory

The endpoint has active code-level callers, but no visible trigger was confirmed on the representative current pages inspected.

Verified active caller definitions include:

- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatboard/chatboard.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatboard/chatboard.pl`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatboard/forum.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatboard/loader.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatboard/newboard.cgi`
- Subject-specific chatboard CGI/Perl trees under:
  - `california`
  - `canada`
  - `australia`
  - `administratorsnet`
  - `mentors`
  - `projects`
  - `states`
  - `pennsylvania`
  - `retired`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/lessons/confirm.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/lessons/submit.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/gazette/discussions.cgi`
- `/var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/optout.cgi`

Many additional references are archived or embedded in historical generated HTML.

## 2. Current chatboard finding

Classification: **A — Current active chatboard dependency**

The active chatboard generators define:

```text
http://teachers.net/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
```

as the mail/contact script and construct `uid` and `dmn` parameters.

The likely user-facing function is a contact-member or contact-author link associated with a post or author record. It is not required for posting, replying, moderation, registration, or rendering the board itself.

The exact visible label was not safely established from the representative public pages inspected.

## 3. Other visible or generated callers

`/var/www/cgi-bin/lessons/confirm.cgi` constructs a mailform link when a lesson has author email metadata. It uses a link pattern equivalent to:

```text
mailform.cgi?uid=...&dmn=...&name=...&subject=Lesson+Bank+...
```

This indicates a possible lesson-author contact trigger, but the inspected current lesson pages displayed ordinary `mailto:` links rather than the legacy mailform URL.

This caller is therefore classified:

**E — Generated reference whose current rendered status requires route-specific confirmation**

`gazette/discussions.cgi` and `optout.cgi` also contain active code references, but no current visible trigger was confirmed.

## 4. Current page inspection

Representative public pages inspected:

- `/chatboard/`
- `/chatboard/topic1/`
- `/classifieds/`
- `/`
- `/lessons/posts/689.html`
- `/lessons/posts/1901.html`
- `/lessonplans/grades/kindergarten/`

Findings:

- No direct `mailform.cgi` link was present in the inspected rendered page output.
- Current lesson pages showed ordinary email links where author contact was visible.
- Current footer/navigation showed general contact, advertising, submissions, and reprints links, not `mailform.cgi`.
- No current classifieds page was confirmed to expose the endpoint.

## 5. Present-day exposure

- Apache logs contain approximately 10,315 `GET` requests to `mailform.cgi` in the retained access-log set.
- Current requests are heavily automated-looking and frequently use malformed or synthetic `uid`/`dmn` values.
- The endpoint is being reached directly by bots or scripted clients.
- No reliable evidence ties the recent abusive requests to a visible current navigation path.
- Active code-level references demonstrate that legitimate callers may still exist, but visible current exposure is not proven from the sampled pages.

## 6. Classified disposition

**D — Ambiguous; perform one narrower route-specific audit**

The endpoint is not safe to retire solely from this audit because active chatboard generators and lesson-generation code still reference it. However, the sampled current pages did not visibly expose it, and current traffic is dominated by direct automated requests.

## 7. Smallest safe next action

Perform one focused, read-only route audit for:

1. A currently active chatboard topic with an author/contact record.
2. A current lesson page whose source metadata includes an author email.
3. A current Gazette discussion page.
4. Any current classifieds route that may contain author contact links.

Inspect generated HTML only; do not submit or activate the links.

After that audit, create a separate approved containment ticket targeting only confirmed active callers.

## Verification

- No mail sent.
- No page, script, endpoint, or configuration modified.
- No queued message modified.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No database write occurred.
- No local repository, DDEV, or Git state changed.
- No private email addresses or message contents appear in this report.

# SEC001 — Legacy Mailform Usage and Dependency Audit

## 1. Reference inventory

Verified reference categories:

- Approximately 7,296 static/generated references under `/var/www/htdocs`
- Approximately 690 references under `/var/www/cgi-bin`
- Numerous archived references under `/var/www/www.teachers.net/cgi-bin/ARcHIVE`
- References occur in:
  - Historical chatboard HTML
  - Current and legacy chatboard CGI/Perl scripts
  - Archived chatboard implementations
  - Legacy generated pages
- No Apache rewrite or alias directive directly targeting `mailform.cgi` was identified.
- The executable endpoint is:

  ```text
  /var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
  ```

- The endpoint invokes `/usr/sbin/sendmail` and constructs recipients from request parameters.

## 2. Current chatboard dependency

Classification: **A — Current active chatboard dependency**

Evidence:

- Current chatboard CGI/Perl trees contain references to the mailform route and its `uid`/`dmn` parameters.
- The endpoint is receiving current production traffic.
- Current requests use:

  ```text
  GET /cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi?... 
  ```

- The endpoint appears to support chatboard “contact member” or contact-related workflows.
- It is not used for posting, replying, moderation, registration, or core chatboard page rendering; those workflows are implemented by other CGI paths.

The exact full set of active chatboard callers remains broad because the production tree contains many historical/generated copies.

## 3. Classifieds dependency

Classification: **F — not conclusively established**

The audit found extensive historical references across generated/static content, but available evidence does not prove that current public classifieds depend on `mailform.cgi`.

Current findings support:

- The endpoint is primarily associated with chatboard/contact workflows.
- Historical classifieds and generated pages may contain inherited references.
- No current classifieds route was safely confirmed as requiring the endpoint.
- A route-level access-log review is still needed before retirement.

## 4. Recent legitimate-use versus abuse evidence

Recent Apache logs show repeated automated-looking requests to `mailform.cgi`:

- Almost entirely `GET` requests
- Many requests clustered closely together
- Caller-controlled `uid`, `dmn`, `name`, and `subject` parameters
- Empty or malformed parameters are accepted
- Repeated browser-like user agents
- Requests return HTTP 200
- Requests are consistent with automated abuse and mail harvesting

A small number of requests may represent genuine contact workflows, but HTTP Referer and user-agent data cannot establish legitimacy. No reliable separation between human and abusive traffic is currently possible from logs alone.

## 5. User-facing retirement impact

| Action | Expected impact |
|---|---|
| Immediate endpoint retirement | Breaks current chatboard contact-member/email links and any undiscovered legacy callers |
| Temporary blocking | Stops abusive mail but also interrupts legitimate contact workflows |
| Preserve form, disable sending | Preserves page behavior but prevents contact delivery |
| Later replacement | Allows validation, rate limiting, abuse controls, and a safer mail path |

## 6. Recommended disposition

**B — Contain immediately, then replace**

The endpoint is demonstrably active, so immediate permanent retirement is not yet safe. However, the current unauthenticated GET-driven mail interface is actively abused and should not remain unrestricted.

## 7. Smallest safe next action

Create a separate implementation ticket limited to:

```text
/var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
```

That ticket should first add reversible containment, such as:

- Rejecting unauthenticated or malformed requests
- Requiring POST for mail submission
- Validating `uid` and `dmn`
- Adding rate limiting and abuse controls
- Preserving a documented allowlist for legitimate chatboard routes

Before implementation, perform one focused route audit for current classifieds and chatboard pages to identify the minimum legitimate workflow that must remain functional.

## Verification

- No mail sent.
- No endpoint or caller modified.
- No queued messages modified.
- No production configuration changed.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No database write occurred.
- No local repository, DDEV, or Git state changed.
- No private email addresses or message contents appear in this report.

# OPS005 — Mail Source Forensic Correlation

## 1. Representative queue timeline

Postfix shows repeated messages from `www-data@teachers.net` at approximately 5–10 minute intervals:

| Queue ID | Sender | Size | Pattern |
|---|---|---:|---|
| `369882001CF` | `www-data@teachers.net` | 7,568 bytes | Recreated/retried repeatedly |
| `A0C91200138` | `www-data@teachers.net` | 7,572 bytes | Recreated/retried repeatedly |
| `BDB06200136` | `www-data@teachers.net` | 7,590 bytes | Recreated/retried repeatedly |
| `5823720013E` | `www-data@teachers.net` | 7,565 bytes | Recreated/retried repeatedly |
| `854AA2001D5` | `www-data@teachers.net` | 7,572 bytes | Recreated/retried repeatedly |

Delivery failures include connection refusal, timeout, IPv6 network failure, and remote throttling.

Exact historical Apache request timestamps for every representative queue ID could not be matched because the relevant access-log interval is rotated/compressed and not fully retained in an easily correlated form.

## 2. Correlated request/process

The exact source component is:

```text
/var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
```

Verified evidence:

- The endpoint is publicly reachable through:

  ```text
  /cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
  ```

- Current Apache logs show repeated unauthenticated `GET` requests to that endpoint with attacker-controlled `uid`, `dmn`, `name`, and `subject` parameters.
- The requests return HTTP 200.
- The script invokes `/usr/sbin/sendmail`.
- The script constructs a recipient from the supplied `uid` and `dmn` values.
- Postfix records the resulting messages as originating from `www-data@teachers.net`.
- The request pattern is automated and includes malformed, empty, and unrelated recipient values.

## 3. Exact source component

The source is the legacy public CGI endpoint:

```text
/var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
```

It is not the WordPress mail stack or a cron process based on the evidence collected.

## 4. Root-cause classification

Primary classification:

**D — Public endpoint being abused**

Secondary classification:

**E — Compromised or abused application path**

The endpoint permits unauthenticated GET-driven mail generation and accepts recipient-related input from the request. This explains the varied external destinations and the repeated `www-data` sender.

The remote `mail.chicagoes.xyz` refusal is a delivery failure, but it is not the root cause of queue generation.

## 5. Smallest safe corrective action

The smallest corrective action should target only:

```text
/var/www/cgi-bin/chatscripts/mailform.cgi
```

Recommended reversible containment, requiring a separate approval ticket:

1. Temporarily block or gate the endpoint’s mail-sending path.
2. Preserve the page and legitimate workflow if possible.
3. Add strict recipient validation, request-method validation, rate limiting, and abuse controls.
4. Review whether the endpoint is still required before restoring mail delivery.

Expected impact: legitimate chatboard “contact member” mail through this endpoint may stop or become restricted until corrected.

## 6. Queue disposition

- Do not flush, delete, or retry the queue before containment.
- After the source is contained, classify queued messages individually or by verified abuse pattern.
- Expire or remove confirmed abusive messages only through a separate approved queue-management ticket.
- Preserve legitimate queued mail where identifiable.
- Monitor whether new `www-data` messages stop after containment.

## 7. Verification plan

After an approved fix:

- Confirm no new queue entries originate from the CGI endpoint.
- Confirm Postfix queue growth stops.
- Confirm legitimate contact-mail behavior through a controlled test.
- Review Apache access logs for continued automated requests.
- Confirm no unrelated WordPress or CGI mail paths were disabled.
- Monitor disk usage and mail queue size for at least one full retry interval.

## Verification

- No mail sent.
- No queued messages modified.
- No production files changed.
- No endpoint disabled.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No database write occurred.
- No local files, DDEV state, or Git state changed.
- No message bodies, credentials, or private records were included.

# OPS004 — Production Mail Queue & Delivery Audit

## 1. Root-cause assessment

Two separate conditions are present:

1. `mail.chicagoes.xyz` resolves to `157.230.96.33`, but TCP port 25 refuses connections. This is primarily:

   **A — Remote server unavailable or refusing SMTP connections.**

2. The queue contains approximately 30 messages addressed across many unrelated, suspicious-looking domains. Most queued messages originate from `www-data@teachers.net`, with repeated approximately 7.5K messages generated at regular intervals.

   This indicates a likely local application or legacy-runtime mail-generation problem, classified as:

   **C/D — Application misconfiguration or stale/compromised process requiring source tracing.**

The queue is not explained solely by the Chicagoes recipient server being unavailable.

## 2. Queue summary

- Queued requests: approximately 30
- Queue size: approximately 323K
- Oldest observed queued item: July 26, 2026 at 04:19
- Several messages have been deferred for multiple days.
- Recipient domains are widely distributed; only a small subset use `chicagoes.xyz`.
- Repeated delivery failures include:
  - Connection refused
  - Connection timeout
  - IPv6 network unreachable
  - Remote `421 Too many concurrent SMTP connections`
- No relay host is configured.
- Postfix is attempting direct-to-MX delivery.

## 3. Source application

Evidence points to a local web-process source:

- Repeated queue entries use sender `www-data@teachers.net`.
- Postfix logs show regular queue creation by the web-service identity.
- The message pattern is consistent with automated application-generated mail rather than a single normal user transaction.
- The exact WordPress plugin, PHP endpoint, CGI script, or scheduled process was not identified in this ticket.

The source remains **unknown**, with a strong lead toward PHP/WordPress or a legacy CGI process running as `www-data`.

## 4. Recommended corrective action

The smallest safe corrective action is a source-identification ticket, not queue deletion:

1. Correlate the Postfix queue-creation timestamps with Apache access logs and application logs.
2. Search reviewed PHP, WordPress, Perl, and CGI code for mail-generation calls.
3. Inspect cron and scheduled application jobs.
4. Check whether a specific endpoint or process is generating the repeated 7.5K messages.
5. Disable or repair only the confirmed source after explicit approval.
6. Handle existing queued messages in a separate, approved queue-retention decision.

Do not flush, delete, or resubmit the queue before the generating source is contained.

## 5. Risk if unresolved

- Continued disk growth under `/var/log` and `/var/spool/postfix`
- Repeated outbound SMTP attempts
- Potential sender/domain reputation damage
- Possible abuse of the web application or legacy CGI runtime
- Increased risk of mail delivery throttling or provider blocking
- Ongoing delivery failures even after the Chicagoes host recovers

## Verification

- No mail sent.
- No queued messages modified.
- No queue flush or deletion occurred.
- No Postfix configuration changed.
- No DNS changes occurred.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No local files, DDEV state, or Git state changed.
- No message bodies or credentials were included in this report.

# OPS003 — Legacy Runtime Tree Attribution Audit

## Inventory

`/var/www/www.teachers.net` consumes approximately 1.22G:

| Directory | Size | Classification |
|---|---:|---|
| `htdocs/` | 923M | E — active dependency |
| `cgi-bin/` | 298M | E — active dependency |

| Directory | Size | Purpose | Class |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `htdocs/wong/` | 736M | Historical Gazette/Wong content and assets | C — preserve |
| `htdocs/gazette/` | 178M | Gazette content and back issues | C — preserve |
| `htdocs/black/` | 8.6M | Legacy content | B/D — retention review |
| Remaining content directories | Less than 1M each | Static legacy routes and assets | E/C — preserve pending route review |

### Major `cgi-bin` consumers

| Directory | Size | Purpose | Class |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `cgi-bin/banner/` | 149M | Advertising/banner runtime and logs | E — active dependency |
| `cgi-bin/ARcHIVE/` | 131M | Archived CGI scripts, indexes, and historical data | B/C — retention review |
| `cgi-bin/chatboard/` | 7.3M | Chatboard runtime | E — active dependency |
| `cgi-bin/advertising/` | 3.5M | Advertising runtime | E — active dependency |
| `cgi-bin/gazette/` | 904K | Gazette CGI runtime | E — active dependency |
| Remaining CGI directories | Less than 1M each | Supporting legacy scripts | D/E — focused review required |

## Composition

Observed contents include:

- 523 `.cgi` files
- 285 Perl `.pl` files
- 6,153 HTML files
- 7,885 log files
- 53 ZIP archives
- Three compressed `.gz` files
- Historical search indexes and Swish-e files
- Advertising logs, including a roughly 52M banner log
- Historical creative assets including PSD, PPT, DOCX, PDF, and image files

## Activity evidence

Numerous `gazcounter.count` files under `htdocs/wong/` were modified on July 28, 2026. This confirms that at least part of the apparently historical Wong tree remains actively written and must be treated as an active dependency.

The presence of archive directories does not prove they are unused. Several symlinks connect current and historical content, including:

- Gazette back-issue links
- Wong month-to-month paths
- CGI aliases inside `ARcHIVE`
- Links crossing into `/www/...` paths

These links make bulk deletion or deduplication unsafe.

## Cleanup opportunities

No category is currently safe to remove solely from this audit.

Potential future candidates:

- Old banner/advertising logs: Class B, only after confirming retention and diagnostic requirements.
- Clearly obsolete archive indexes: Class B/D, only after route and symlink dependency review.
- Duplicate historical creative assets: Class D, requiring content-level comparison and owner approval.
- Unused CGI archives: Class D, requiring Apache rewrite and runtime-reference verification.

Estimated immediately recoverable space: **0 bytes safely authorized**.

Possible recoverable space after focused review: **tens to hundreds of megabytes**, primarily from logs or verified duplicate historical assets. This is not an approved estimate for deletion.

## Risks

- `wong/` is actively updated despite its historical appearance.
- `ARcHIVE/` contains executable CGI/Perl code and search indexes.
- Symlinks cross directory boundaries.
- Log files are numerous and may be operational or legal records.
- Static content may be referenced by rewrite rules or legacy URLs.
- Removing archived assets could break historical pages and redirects.

## Recommended read-only follow-up

1. Map Apache rewrite and alias references into `wong`, `gazette`, `ARcHIVE`, and `banner`.
2. Identify which log files are covered by logrotate and their retention requirements.
3. Trace symlink targets before any archive or duplicate review.
4. Separate executable CGI/Perl code from historical data.
5. Produce a route-level dependency map before considering cleanup.
6. Do not transfer or delete this tree as part of the initial WordPress compatibility lab.

## Verification

- No production files changed.
- No production configuration changed.
- No files deleted, moved, compressed, or modified.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No database activity occurred.
- No local files, DDEV state, or Git state changed.

- Root filesystem `/`: 24G total, 20G used, 3.4G available, 86% used
- `/boot`: 881M total, 23% used

## 2. Largest identified consumers

|---|---:|---|
| `/home/ubuntu` | 2.9G | Largest identified top-level directory; traversal timed out before attribution |
| `/var/www/www.teachers.net` | 1.2G | Legacy/application tree; requires focused review |
| `/var/lib` | 1.1G | Includes MariaDB, APT, Snap |
| `/var/log` | 786M | Journal and Apache logs dominate |
| `/var/www/gazette` | 768M | Gazette runtime/content tree |
| `/var/www/htdocs/wp-content` | 696M | WordPress content |
| WordPress uploads | 450M | User/media content |
| MariaDB storage | 377M | Database files |
| `/var/www/cgi-bin` | 263M | Legacy CGI/Perl application |
| `/var/log/journal` | 448M | Journal accounting reported 199.5M active/archive usage |
| `/var/log/apache2` | 259M | Access/error logs |
| WordPress log archive | 104M | Includes 93M `debug-sql.log` archive |
| `/var/lib/apt` | 193M | Package metadata/cache area |
| `/var/backups` | 2.9M | Small |
| `/tmp` | 380K | Small |
| `/var/tmp` | 40K | Small |

## 3. Evidence of abnormal growth

- Root usage at 86% is operationally significant.
- WordPress debug archives include:
  - `debug-sql.log`: approximately 93M
  - `debug.log`: approximately 15M
- Apache logs contain sizeable rotated files, including approximately:
  - 62M `other_vhosts_access.log.1`
  - 50M `sandbox-teachers-ssl-access.log.1`
  - 23M `sandbox-teachers-ssl-error.log.1`
- Systemd journal is substantial but not obviously runaway; usage is approximately 199.5M.
- Two 2015 BackWPup archives remain under uploads, each approximately 9.6M.
- Mail queue contains 29 requests / approximately 312K. Delivery is failing to `mail.chicagoes.xyz` with connection refusal, indicating an active mail-delivery problem rather than a cleanup-only issue.
- No deleted-but-open files were reported.
- Full attribution of `/home/ubuntu`, `/var/www/www.teachers.net`, and `/var/www/htdocs` was bounded but timed out; those remain unresolved large consumers.

## 4. Cleanup-candidate classification

| Candidate | Class | Estimated space | Recommendation |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Old WordPress debug/debug-SQL archives | B — retention confirmation | Up to 104M | Confirm diagnostic/legal retention, then archive or remove old copies |
| Rotated Apache logs | B — retention confirmation | Potentially 100M+ | Verify logrotate retention and incident requirements before removal |
| Systemd journal | B — retention confirmation | Up to approximately 199M | Set a documented size/time policy, then vacuum only under approval |
| 2015 BackWPup archives | B/C — backup verification required | Approximately 19M for two visible files | Preserve until backup/rollback coverage is confirmed |
| WordPress caches | A in principle | Currently approximately 4K | No meaningful recovery currently |
| Wordfence runtime data | B/C | Approximately 10M plus GeoIP data | Do not delete security state without Wordfence/retention review |
| Uploads/media | C — preserve | 450M | User content; do not remove as cleanup |
| MariaDB data | C — preserve | 377M | Do not delete or optimize destructively in this ticket |
| `/home/ubuntu` | D — unknown | 2.9G | Requires bounded, privacy-safe attribution |
| `/var/www/www.teachers.net` | D — unknown | 1.2G | Legacy application/records; requires focused review |
| Mail queue | E — active problem | 312K | Investigate delivery/configuration; do not purge blindly |
| APT metadata/cache | B | Up to approximately 193M | Confirm package rollback needs before package-cache cleanup |

## 5. Recommended low-risk sequence

1. Do not delete user uploads, database files, CGI content, Gazette content, or backup archives.
2. Review and correct WordPress debug logging and retention.
3. Review Apache logrotate configuration and retain logs required for operations/legal/audit purposes.
4. Establish a systemd journal size/time limit; perform cleanup only after approval.
5. Audit `/home/ubuntu` with a bounded, privacy-safe inventory.
6. Audit `/var/www/www.teachers.net` and `/var/www/htdocs` for historical archives, exports, and generated data.
7. Verify backup recoverability before considering old backup removal.
8. Investigate the mail queue and failed destination; do not treat queue deletion as the fix.
9. Review APT cache retention only after confirming package rollback requirements.

A conservative first-pass target is 300–500M, primarily from confirmed-obsolete logs and journal data. This would raise free space to approximately 3.7–3.9G, or 15–16%. The longer-term target should be at least 20% free space.

## 6. Configuration safeguards

- Enforce bounded WordPress debug-log retention.
- Verify Apache `logrotate` policies and alert on rotation failures.
- Set explicit systemd journal limits.
- Define backup retention tiers: operational, rollback, and historical/legal.
- Set cache size/age limits where caches are confirmed disposable.
- Add disk alerts at 80%, 85%, and 90%.
- Add inode alerts at 70%, 80%, and 90%.
- Generate periodic read-only disk reports.
- Consider AWS volume expansion if usage remains above 80% after approved cleanup or projected growth indicates less than 30 days of headroom.

  - `wp-recaptcha-integration`
- WordPress is multisite/subdirectory-based.
- Database tables use the `wp_` prefix plus numbered multisite prefixes such as `wp_6_` and `wp_9_`.

## 2. Runtime-zone map

| Zone | Verified paths/behavior |
|---|---|
| WordPress/PHP | `/var/www/htdocs`, served by the primary HTTPS vhost |
| WordPress uploads/media | `/var/www/htdocs/wp-content/uploads` |
| WordPress caches/runtime data | `wp-content/cache`, `w3tc-config`, `wflogs`, backup/plugin runtime directories |
| Legacy CGI/Perl | `/var/www/cgi-bin`, `.cgi` and `.pl` handlers |
| Additional CGI | `/var/www/www.teachers.net/cgi-bin` |
| Gazette | Separate Apache vhost and document root with CGI |
| SSI/static | `chatrooms/one`, `lesson*`, and `/wong/` have `Includes` enabled |
| Rewrite-driven legacy routes | HTML requests route to CGI wrappers such as `newwrapper.cgi`, `classifieds-theme.cgi`, `lessonz-theme.cgi`, and Gazette handlers |
| Jobs | `jobs.teachers.net` configuration exists in `sites-available`; it was not shown as an active vhost in the active Apache listing |

## 3. Dependency and boundary table

| Dependency | Evidence | Local classification |
|---|---|---|
| Apache virtual hosts and rewrites | Active `000-teachers.net*` and Gazette configs | Required for compatibility lab |
| WordPress/PHP | `/var/www/htdocs`, PHP 8.3, WordPress 6.8.6 | Required |
| Multisite database | `wp_`, `wp_6_`, `wp_9_` tables | Required |
| Custom WordPress plugins/themes | Production `wp-content` inventory | Required for WordPress fidelity |
| MU plugins | `/var/www/htdocs/wp-content/mu-plugins` exists | Required; must be inventoried before transfer |
| CGI/Perl | Extensive `/var/www/cgi-bin` tree and CGI handlers | Required for legacy route fidelity |
| SSI | Apache `Includes` on legacy paths | Required for affected legacy areas |
| Gazette runtime | Separate root and CGI tree | Optional initially; required for Gazette fidelity |
| Mail | `sendmail`, `postfix`, and `mail` binaries present | Production dependency; stub/disable locally |
| Uploads | `wp-content/uploads` is writable and populated | Optional for initial lab; later fidelity |
| Writable CGI and web roots | `www-data` writable permissions observed | Production risk; use controlled local directories |
| Cron | No matching Teachers.Net entries found in inspected user/system cron locations | Unknown; requires focused service/cron audit |
| Symlink boundaries | Full inspection did not complete within the safe command window | Unknown |
| `.htaccess` | No `.htaccess` paths were returned under `/var/www` | Verify separately before transfer |
| PHP custom include paths/extensions | Inspection command timed out before completion | Unknown |

## 4. Local-replica requirements

### Required for initial compatibility lab

- Independent DDEV project and database
- WordPress multisite structure
- Production-compatible PHP version, preferably PHP 8.3 initially
- Custom theme, plugins, and MU plugins
- Apache rewrite/alias behavior
- CGI/Perl compatibility boundary
- SSI-enabled legacy paths
- Sanitized database

### Optional for later fidelity

- Complete uploads/media tree
- Gazette vhost and content
- Historical/archived CGI trees
- Production cache and backup directories, which should normally be excluded

### Production-only and locally stubbed

- Real outbound mail
- Production cron jobs
- Live payment, authentication, advertising, analytics, and external integrations
- Wordfence/live security telemetry
- Production certificates and secrets

### Unknown; later focused audit

- Exact cron dependencies
- Symlink targets crossing root boundaries
- Perl module inventory
- PHP-FPM/runtime configuration
- Exact `.htaccess` status
- CGI scripts that invoke external binaries or databases
- Non-WordPress database dependencies

## 5. Risks and unknowns

- Production is a hybrid Apache/WordPress/CGI/Perl/SSI application, not a WordPress-only site.
- A standard DDEV WordPress image will not reproduce the complete runtime.
- The existing production web root and CGI root are broad, writable application areas.
- The existing local `teachers-net-site` workspace must remain isolated.
- Production database contents include multisite and legacy plugin tables; import requires sanitization and URL rewriting.
- A full production clone may contain private data, credentials, logs, backups, and security state.
- Some inspection commands timed out while traversing large production trees; those areas remain explicitly unknown.

## 6. Architecture refinement

ARCH001’s separate-project recommendation is confirmed and strengthened:

```text
/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-site/   # existing engineering workspace
/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/   # isolated production compatibility lab
```

The new project should initially be a WordPress/PHP compatibility lab with a documented legacy-runtime boundary. CGI/Perl/SSI should be added only through a separately scoped implementation ticket.

Recommended initial DDEV baseline:

- Project name: `teachers-net-live`
- PHP: 8.3
- Separate database
- Separate hostname: `teachers-net-live.ddev.site`
- Mail disabled or captured by Mailpit
- No production cron, outbound mail, certificates, or secrets
- No direct reuse of the existing `teachers-net` DDEV project

## 7. Smallest safe next implementation ticket

Create an empty, independent `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live` DDEV project configured for WordPress multisite and PHP 8.3 only.

That ticket should:

- Create no production connection
- Transfer no production files or database
- Avoid CGI, Perl, SSI, uploads, and custom plugins initially
- Verify independent DDEV routing and database identity
- Leave `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-site` untouched

A later bounded audit should inspect cron, symlinks, PHP-FPM configuration, Perl modules, and CGI external dependencies.

## Verification

- No production files changed.
- No production configuration changed.
- No service restarted or reloaded.
- No database write or export occurred.
- No local files changed.
- No DDEV state changed.
- No Git state changed.
- Existing Job Center workspace remained untouched.
- No credentials are included in this report.

Note: an initial read-only configuration inspection used an incomplete redaction pattern and emitted sensitive key material to the internal command output. It did not write or modify production, and the material is not reproduced here.

### Readiness decision

The lab is ready for routine Job Center front-end development and route-level QA, but not yet for reliable authenticated employer workflow QA.

### Critical route matrix

| Route | Result | Finding |
|---|---:|---|
| `/` | 200 | Loads; legacy production asset references remain |
| `/wp-admin/` | 200/login | Local authentication boundary works |
| `/jobs/` | 200 | Loads with Flatroller |
| `/jobs/search/` | 200 | Loads |
| `/jobs/job-seekers/` | 200 | Loads |
| `/jobs/my-dashboard/` | 200/login | Correct local login redirect |
| `/jobs/submit-job/` | 200/login | Correct local login redirect |
| `/jobs/edit-job/` | 200/login | Correct local login redirect |
| Representative topic | 200 | `/topic/welcome-to-the-teacher-chatboard/` |

### Breakage inventory

- Hardcoded `teachers.net` asset/feed/social URLs: Blocks visual fidelity — Class B.
- Job Center employer routes require authentication: expected behavior, but authenticated workflow remains unverified — Class F.
- No production uploads were synchronized: visual/media fidelity limitation — Class B/C.
- No published job records were available for a real job-detail test — Class F.
- PHP 8.3 emits legacy JobRoller/Flatroller deprecation warnings and notices — Class C unless they affect a specific workflow.
- No confirmed missing local CSS/JS files in the sampled Job Center page.
- No CGI/Perl/SSI dependency encountered in Job Center routes.
- No redirect to production was observed in route navigation, though page markup still contains production URLs.
- AJAX and authenticated form submission remain unverified without a local test account.

### Local-only fixes applied

No new broad code changes were made. Existing local compatibility fixes remain:

- WordPress multisite rewrite rules.
- `/chatboard/` local alias to `/chatboards/`.
- Local cookie-domain handling.
- Local multisite URL/configuration and disabled WP-Cron.
- Mailpit sendmail routing.

### Upload/media recommendation

Use a small selected upload subset or placeholders for Job Center visual QA. Full uploads synchronization is unnecessary at this stage and would copy unrelated/private production media.

### Remaining blockers

1. Create/use a local test account for employer authentication.
2. Add a small representative set of Job Center media.
3. Obtain or create a safe representative job record for detail/edit-flow testing.
4. Triage hardcoded production asset URLs selectively; do not perform a broad replacement.

### Recommended next ticket

`LOCAL006 — Local Job Center Test Account and Representative Workflow Fixture`

Sandy, production, `/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-site`, and other repositories were untouched. No Git repository was initialized in the local lab.

## 1. Local lab confirmation

## 3. Classified transfer manifest

| Item | Classification | Handling |
|---|---|---|
| WordPress core | A | Use local 6.8.6 baseline; verify before import |
| `wp-config.php` | C/D | Do not copy; retain DDEV config and recreate safe local settings |
| Active theme `twentysixteen-boss` | A | Transfer after checksum and compatibility review |
| Other themes | B | Transfer only if required for multisite/site fidelity |
| Active plugins | A | Transfer selectively and activate only after review |
| Inactive plugins | B/D | Exclude initially; add only when dependency is proven |
| MU plugins | A if present | Inventory again at full depth; transfer only reviewed files |
| Uploads | B | Optional later; exclude initially |
| Caches | D | Exclude and regenerate |
| Logs | D | Exclude |
| Backup directories | D | Exclude |
| Wordfence runtime data | D | Do not copy; regenerate locally |
| Database | A | Export, sanitize, and import separately |
| Cron state | C | Disable local cron; recreate only approved schedules |
| Mail configuration | C | Use DDEV/Mailpit; never copy production mail settings |
| Analytics/advertising | C | Stub or disable locally |
| Certificates/secrets | D | Never copy; recreate safe local values |
| CGI/Perl/SSI/Gazette | E | Separate compatibility workstream; excluded from this transfer |

## 4. Database export and sanitization plan

Recommended production export, only in a later approved ticket:

```bash
ssh sandy 'mkdir -p /home/ubuntu/forensics/local-transfer'
ssh sandy 'wp --path=/var/www/htdocs db export /home/ubuntu/forensics/local-transfer/teachers-net.sql --add-drop-table --all-tables-with-prefix --allow-root'
ssh sandy 'gzip -9 /home/ubuntu/forensics/local-transfer/teachers-net.sql'
ssh sandy 'sha256sum /home/ubuntu/forensics/local-transfer/teachers-net.sql.gz'
```

Transfer only after checksum capture:

```bash
rsync -avz --dry-run \
  sandy:/home/ubuntu/forensics/local-transfer/teachers-net.sql.gz \
  /home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/backups/
```

Then perform the approved transfer and verify the checksum.

Import locally:

```bash
ddev import-db --src=/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/backups/teachers-net.sql.gz
```

Sanitize locally using serialized-data-safe WP-CLI replacement:

```bash
ddev wp search-replace \
  'https://teachers.net' \
  'https://teachers-net-live.ddev.site' \
  --all-tables-with-prefix \
  --precise \
  --recurse-objects \
  --skip-columns=guid \
  --dry-run
```

Run the non-dry replacement only after review.

Local safety requirements:

- Set `DISABLE_WP_CRON` to `true`.
- Route mail to Mailpit.
- Disable outbound integrations, analytics, advertising, payment, and security telemetry.
- Recreate secrets locally.
- Preserve private-data handling and restrict local access.
- Retain the original dump only until verification and rollback approval, then securely remove temporary copies.

## 5. File transfer plan

Active theme:

```bash
rsync -avz --dry-run --checksum \
  --exclude='*.log' \
  --exclude='cache/' \
  sandy:/var/www/htdocs/wp-content/themes/twentysixteen-boss/ \
  /home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/themes/twentysixteen-boss/
```

Required plugins:


```bash

  sandy:/var/www/htdocs/wp-content/mu-plugins/ \
  /home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/
```

Optional selected uploads:

```bash
rsync -avz --dry-run --checksum \
  --exclude='cache/' \
  --exclude='backwpup-*/' \
  --exclude='wflogs/' \
  sandy:/var/www/htdocs/wp-content/uploads/sites/6/ \
  /home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/sites/6/
```

Rules:

- Always run `--dry-run` first.
- Do not use `--delete` initially.
- Do not copy ownership from production.
- Normalize local ownership through DDEV.
- Verify file counts and checksums after transfer.
- Roll back by removing only the transferred component or restoring the clean local baseline.

## 6. Transfer order

1. Preserve the clean local lab baseline.
2. Record local and production checksums/manifests.
3. Transfer reviewed active theme and required plugins.
4. Transfer MU plugins only after full inventory review.
5. Export and checksum the production database.
6. Transfer and import the database locally.
7. Perform serialized-safe URL replacement.
8. Disable cron, mail, secrets, analytics, advertising, and external integrations.
9. Verify multisite behavior and active theme/plugin compatibility.
10. Add selected uploads only if required.
11. Keep CGI, Perl, SSI, Gazette, and legacy Apache behavior outside this milestone.

- WordPress versions match at 6.8.6.
- Production PHP is 8.3.6; local PHP is 8.3.31.

[ASSISTANT] item-163 turn=019fadf4-ed20-74c2-9c33-e54b769fe15b
The code-level cause is now clear: the feed compares a post’s `local_id` (the `tnet_local_data.path_id`, e.g. AI board `241`) against the user’s membership list (`tnet_groups.group_id`, e.g. `227`). Those identifiers are different namespaces, so the feed star cannot become active even when `tnet_memberships` confirms membership. I’m doing one final read-only comparison and recording the exact rollback boundary.

[ASSISTANT] item-164 turn=019fadf4-ed20-74c2-9c33-e54b769fe15b
CHAT001 diagnostic complete. No files or database records were changed.

- Board: AI in Education
  - `tnet_groups.group_id = 227`
  - `tnet_local_data.path_id = 241`
- Authoritative membership: `tnet_memberships`, requiring `group_id=227`, `user_id=<current user>`, `status=1`.
- Local evidence: one active membership exists for group 227, user ID 1. The synthetic account `localemployerqa` is user 77722 and has no membership, so the exact reported account state cannot be reproduced locally.

Root cause confirmed in the main feed:

`page-chatcenter.php:422-428` compares:

```php
$tnetpost->local_id
```

against:

```php
$local_data->groups
```

The former is a `tnet_local_data.path_id` such as `241`; the latter contains `tnet_groups.group_id` such as `227`. Because these are different identifier namespaces, the feed’s `starjoin member` class is never set.

The detail/header view works differently and correctly resolves `local_path` to `group_id` before calling `groupclick_function()` in `header-boardinset.php:34-46`.

Secondary issue: the feed member-count query correctly counts active `tnet_memberships`, but writes to object-style `$feed->local_id` while rendering reads array-style `$feed['local_id']` (`page-chatcenter.php:648-679` versus `468-473`). Counts may therefore also be absent.

Smallest fix: normalize the feed comparison through the existing `groupMap`/`tnet_groups` mapping, or call the shared membership check with the resolved `group_id`. Do not alter membership data.

Verification matrix:

- Detail/header star: authoritative `tnet_memberships` check — code path verified.
- Main feed star: path ID versus group ID mismatch — failure confirmed.
- Member count: query source verified; storage/render shape mismatch identified.
- Cache: no relevant transient or object-cache dependency found.
- Production: not accessed or modified.
- Git: local lab is not a Git repository; no CHAT001 files changed.
- Rollback: none required; rollback boundary is limited to any future code-only feed normalization.

### Diagnoses and fixes

1. **bbPress translation notice**

- Origin: third-party bbPress/BuddyPress bridge during `bp_include`.
- Cause: `BBP_Forums_Component::__construct()` calls translation functions before `init`.
- Local fix: added [`local012-runtime-compat.php`](/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/local012-runtime-compat.php), which disables only the legacy bbPress/BuddyPress bridge locally. Basic bbPress topic rendering remains available.
- Production status: not approved or deployed.

2. **`wpdb::prepare()` notice**

- Origin: imported custom theme [`functions-tracker.php`](/home/bobreap/projects/teachers-net-live/wordpress/wp-content/themes/twentysixteen-boss/functions-tracker.php), `tnet516_create_tnetuser()`.
- Cause: `$wpdb->insert()` received `NULL` with a `%d` format, resulting in a placeholder-less `prepare()` call.
- Local fix: NULL visitor IDs now use an equivalent literal `INSERT ... VALUES (NULL)`; non-NULL IDs retain the existing prepared insert path.
- Production status: not approved or deployed.

### Verification

- WP_DEBUG remains enabled.
- Both target notices are absent after repeated requests to root, chatboards, topic, holding, admin, and redirected `/jobs/` routes.
- No new warnings, deprecations, or fatals were observed in responses.

Summary:

- Current local mail path: DDEV Mailpit only; no active chatboard sender was found.
- Missing: event ledger, delayed queue, moderation gates, throttling, suppression, bounce/complaint handling, digest worker, audit trail, and kill switch.
- Recommended architecture: WordPress-native queued notification service with a replaceable provider transport.
- Recommended pilot: explicit re-consent, direct replies to a user’s own topic, Mailpit first, production dry-run, manual approval, 30-minute hold, strict daily/site caps, unsubscribe, and emergency pause.
- Safeguards cover spam, moderation delays, coded/salacious content, mass joins, reposts, duplicate sends, fatigue, bounces, complaints, and deliverability ramp.
- No notifications enabled, no email sent, no queue records created, no preferences or memberships changed, and no production access occurred.

Recommended next ticket: Phase 0 schema/consent/m moderation-state design, still documentation-only until the sender and production boundary are explicitly approved.

## Generic Hopper Processing Instructions

1. Identify every local file modified by the ticket that should be included in the handoff.

2. Include only files authorized by the ticket and relevant to the next-session context.

3. Before adding new active files:
   - Create an `archive/` directory inside the hopper if needed.
   - Move every existing active hopper file into `archive/`.
   - Rename each archived file with a UTC timestamp prefix.
   - Preserve archived files; do not delete them.

4. Keep all current handoff files directly at the hopper root. Do not nest active files in source-path directories.

5. Preserve engineer-owned exceptions exactly as instructed. Never archive, edit, overwrite, rename, copy, manifest, or report protected files.

6. Copy each new handoff file into the hopper root.

7. Update `HOPPER-MANIFEST.txt` with:
   - active source paths;
   - active hopper filenames;
   - archive batch timestamp;
   - protected-file exclusions.

8. Verify:
   - active files are all at one level;
   - archived files have timestamped names;
   - copied files match their source files;
   - protected files remain untouched;
   - no unrelated files were changed.

9. Every post-ticket report involving hopper creation or refresh must include:
   - a link to the hopper directory;
   - a separate bullet list naming every modified file included in the current hopper.

10. Hopper files are handoff copies only. The repository remains the source of truth.

Updated the durable hopper procedure.

New rule:

- When a file is created or refreshed, create the active root copy and its timestamped archive duplicate in the same step.
- Archive path: `\\teachers-net-site\\tmp\\hopper\\archive\\<project-name>\\`
- Archive filename format: `filename-YYMMDDHHMMSS.ext`
- Future refreshes delete only obsolete active document files.
- Never move or rename active files later.

[ASSISTANT] item-310 turn=019fb335-cf6c-7c03-adb5-80213c4bb717
## Standard Hopper Procedure

### Standard directory

Use this directory for every project:

```text
\teachers-net-site\tmp\hopper
```

All projects share this hopper. Each project has its own archive directory.

Example:

```text
\teachers-net-site\tmp\hopper\
├── active handoff files
├── output.txt
├── HOPPER-MANIFEST.txt
└── archive\
    ├── tnet-3.0\
    ├── lessonbank-2.1\
    └── birdmart\
```

For this project, use:

```text
\teachers-net-site\tmp\hopper\archive\tnet-3.0\
```

### Refresh procedure

1. Identify the active project.

2. Identify every local file modified by the ticket that should be included in the handoff.

3. Before copying new files, move only that project’s current active hopper files into:

   ```text
   \teachers-net-site\tmp\hopper\archive\<project-name>\
   ```

4. Prefix each archived filename with a UTC timestamp, for example:

   ```text
   20260730T131731Z__CT-JC005-report.md
   ```

5. Leave active files belonging to other projects untouched.

6. Copy the new or updated handoff files directly into the hopper root:

   ```text
   \teachers-net-site\tmp\hopper\
   ```

   Do not nest active files under project or source-path directories.

7. Treat `output.txt` as engineer-owned:
   - never archive it;
   - never edit it;
   - never overwrite it;
   - never rename it;
   - never copy it;
   - never include it in the manifest;
   - never include it in the handoff file list;
   - leave it at its exact existing path.

8. Update `HOPPER-MANIFEST.txt` with:
   - active project name;
   - source path for each active file;
   - active hopper filename for each file;
   - archive directory used;
   - archive timestamp batch;
   - protected-file exclusion for `output.txt`.

9. Verify:
   - all active handoff files are directly in the hopper root;
   - only the current project’s files were archived;
   - archived files are timestamp-versioned;
   - copied files match their source files;
   - `output.txt` remains untouched;
   - unrelated project files remain untouched.

### Required post-ticket report

Every post-ticket report involving hopper creation or refresh must include:

- a link to the standard hopper directory;
- a separate bullet list of every modified file copied into the current hopper;
- the project-specific archive directory used;
- confirmation that `output.txt` was excluded and preserved.

The repository remains the source of truth. Hopper files are handoff copies only.

[ASSISTANT] item-308 turn=019fb332-01c8-7fa1-a894-d91bea8466f8
## Standard Hopper Procedure

1. Use one standard hopper directory for all projects.

2. Within that directory, maintain a separate archive for each project:

   ```text
   <standard-hopper-directory>/
   ├── active files
   ├── archive/
   │   ├── <project-a>/
   │   ├── <project-b>/
   │   └── <project-c>/
   └── HOPPER-MANIFEST.txt
   ```

3. Keep all current handoff files directly at the hopper root for easy drag-and-drop.

4. Before each refresh for a project:
   - identify the project;
   - move only that project’s current active documents into `archive/<project>/`;
   - prefix archived filenames with a UTC timestamp;
   - leave active documents belonging to other projects untouched.

5. Copy the new or updated handoff documents directly into the hopper root.

6. Treat `output.txt` as engineer-owned:
   - never archive it;
   - never edit, overwrite, rename, or copy it;
   - never include it in the manifest or handoff file list;
   - leave it at its exact existing path.

7. Update `HOPPER-MANIFEST.txt` with:
   - project name;
   - active source paths;
   - active hopper filenames;
   - archive project and timestamp;
   - protected-file exclusions.

8. Verify:
   - all active files are at one root level;
   - only the current project’s files were archived;
   - archived files are timestamp-versioned;
   - copied files match their source files;
   - `output.txt` is unchanged;
   - unrelated project files remain untouched.

9. Every hopper-related post-ticket report must include:
   - a link to the standard hopper directory;
   - a separate bullet list of every modified file copied into the current hopper;
   - the project-specific archive location used.

The repository remains the source of truth. Hopper files are handoff copies only.

   - a link to the hopper directory;
   - a separate bullet list of every modified file copied into the current hopper.

2. Save it directly in the shared hopper root as:


3. Create a same-cycle duplicate under:

   ```text
   archive/<project-name>/
   ```

4. Include the report filename/link in the final response.
5. The report must separately list every modified file included in the hopper.
6. Clarify that the file is generated from the final report content, not captured automatically from the rendered interface.

C3-PUB002 — Community Rich Composer Foundation

Execution mode

Bounded implementation using the existing Community publisher architecture.

Goal

Transform the current text-only composer into the architectural foundation for the mature Community publisher without implementing rich media, uploads, feeds, or notifications.

Scope

Implement:

- Modular composer layout.
- Attachment framework (empty state).
- Link attachment framework (no enrichment yet).
- Post mode framework (Question, Discussion, Candle, Idea placeholders only).
- Extensible attachment area.
- Publisher pipeline prepared for future media families.
- Accessibility and keyboard support.
- Progressive disclosure UI (advanced controls collapsed by default).

Do not implement:

- Image upload.
- Video upload.
- Audio upload.
- PDFs.
- Office documents.
- Open Graph fetching.
- Twitter/X Cards.
- oEmbed.
- Polls.
- Events.
- Feed cards.
- Notifications.
- AI.
- Storage changes beyond required attachment abstractions.
- Production deployment.

Browser review page

Page/Feature:
Community Rich Composer Foundation

Review URL:
https://teachers-net.ddev.site/community/new/

Acceptance

The composer should feel like a modern publishing surface while still publishing ordinary text topics exactly as today.

The architecture must clearly accommodate future attachment types without redesigning the composer.

Documentation

Create:

- community-rich-composer-foundation-v1.md
- community-rich-composer-qa-v1.md

Update:

- Community Project Cursor
- Community Engineering Handoff

Stop boundary

- foundation UI

- publisher compatibility
- browser QA

- validated hopper

Do not begin link enrichment or media implementation.

Completion report
